    - Replace norcalli/nvim-colorizer.lua with NvChad/nvim-colorizer.lua
    - Remove obsolete popup.nvim and unused cmp submodules
    - Fix Treesitter universal FileType activation and file-size guard
    - Fix updatetime = 500 in autocmds and remove duplicate GoFormat
    - Improve keymaps: Esc clears search, cabbrev for terminal shortcuts, and scope LSP keymaps to LspAttach
    - Fix LSP server configs: luals workspace library, pyright completion conflict, and invalid filetypes
    - Centralize DAP config in after/plugin/dap.lua
    - Format Lua codebase with stylua

21:   local mode = vim.api.nvim_get_mode().mode
22: 
23:   if mode == 'c' or vim.fn.getcmdwintype() ~= '' then return config.cmdline.enabled end
24:   if mode == 't' then return config.term.enabled end
25: 
26:   -- Disable in macros
27:   if vim.fn.reg_recording() ~= '' or vim.fn.reg_executing() ~= '' then return false end
28: 
29:   local user_enabled = config.enabled
30:   if type(user_enabled) == 'function' then user_enabled = user_enabled() end
31:   -- User explicitly ignores default conditions
32:   if user_enabled == 'force' then return true end
33: 
34:   -- Buffer explicitly set completion to true, always enable
35:   if user_enabled and vim.b.completion == true then return true end
36: 
37:   -- Buffer explicitly set completion to false, always disable
38:   if vim.b.completion == false then return false end
39:

**Investigating Fuzzy Matcher Implementation**

The code checks if a native library, presumably for fuzzy matching, is available. If it's not, and the configuration *prefers* the native implementation, a warning is logged indicating fallback to a Lua implementation. This indicates a preference for a native fuzzy matching algorithm. The fallback suggests that the Lua version is always available as a last resort.

<h1 align="center">

</h1>

The `nvim-treesitter` plugin provides
1. functions for installing, updating, and removing [**tree-sitter parsers**](SUPPORTED_LANGUAGES.md);
2. a collection of **queries** for enabling tree-sitter features built into Neovim for these languages;
3. a staging ground for [treesitter-based features](#Supported-features) considered for upstreaming to Neovim.

For details on these and how to help improving them, see [CONTRIBUTING.md](./CONTRIBUTING.md).

>[!CAUTION]
> This is a full, incompatible, rewrite: Treat this as a different plugin you need to set up from scratch following the instructions below. If you can't or don't want to update, specify the [`master` branch](https://github.com/nvim-treesitter/nvim-treesitter/blob/master/README.md) (which is locked but will remain available for backward compatibility with Nvim 0.11).

# Quickstart

## Requirements

- Neovim 0.12.0 or later (nightly)
- `tar` and `curl` in your path
- [`tree-sitter-cli`](https://github.com/tree-sitter/tree-sitter/blob/master/crates/cli/README.md) (0.26.1 or later, installed via your package manager, **not npm**)
- a C compiler in your path (see <https://docs.rs/cc/latest/cc/#compile-time-requirements>)

>[!IMPORTANT]
> The current **support policy** for Neovim is
> * the _latest_ [stable release](https://github.com/neovim/neovim/releases/tag/stable),
> * the _latest_ [nightly prerelease](https://github.com/neovim/neovim/releases/tag/nightly).
> Other versions may work but are neither tested nor considered for fixes.

## Installation

You can install `nvim-treesitter` with your favorite package manager (or using the native `package` feature of vim, see `:h packages`).

This plugin is only guaranteed to work with specific versions of language parsers** (as specified in the `parser.lua` table). **When upgrading the plugin, you must make sure that all installed parsers are updated to the latest version** via `:TSUpdate`.
It is strongly recommended to automate this; e.g., using the following spec with [lazy.nvim](https://github.com/folke/lazy.nvim):

```lua
{
  'nvim-treesitter/nvim-treesitter',
  lazy = false,

>[!IMPORTANT]
> This plugin does not support lazy-loading.

## Setup

`nvim-treesitter` can be configured by calling `setup`. **You do not need to call `setup` for `nvim-treesitter` to work using default values.**

```lua
require('nvim-treesitter').setup {
  -- Directory to install parsers and queries to (prepended to `runtimepath` to have priority)
  install_dir = vim.fn.stdpath('data') .. '/site'
}
```

Parsers and queries can then be installed with

```lua
require('nvim-treesitter').install { 'rust', 'javascript', 'zig' }
```

(This is a no-op if the parsers are already installed.) Note that this function runs asynchronously; for synchronous installation in a script context ("bootstrapping"), you need to `wait()` for it to finish:

```lua
require('nvim-treesitter').install({ 'rust', 'javascript', 'zig' }):wait(300000) -- wait max. 5 minutes
```

Check [`:h nvim-treesitter-commands`](doc/nvim-treesitter.txt) for a list of all available commands.

# Supported languages

For `nvim-treesitter` to support a specific feature for a specific language requires both a parser for that language and an appropriate language-specific query file for that feature.

A list of the currently supported languages can be found [on this page](SUPPORTED_LANGUAGES.md). If you wish to add a new language or improve the queries for an existing one, please see our [contributing guide](CONTRIBUTING.md).

# Supported features

`nvim-treesitter` provides queries for the following features. **These are not automatically enabled.**

## Highlighting

Treesitter highlighting is provided by Neovim, see `:h treesitter-highlight`. To enable it for a filetype, put `vim.treesitter.start()` in a `ftplugin/<filetype>.lua` in your config directory, or place the following in your `init.lua`:

```lua
vim.api.nvim_create_autocmd('FileType', {
  pattern = { '<filetype>' },
  callback = function() vim.treesitter.start() end,
})
```

## Folds

Treesitter-based folding is provided by Neovim. To enable it, put the following in your `ftplugin` or `FileType` autocommand:

```lua
vim.wo[0][0].foldexpr = 'v:lua.vim.treesitter.foldexpr()'
vim.wo[0][0].foldmethod = 'expr'

Thinking **Investigating Submodule Branch Updates** The script executes `git submodule update --re... 
**Investigating Submodule Branch Updates**

The script executes `git submodule update --remote`. Examining the `.gitmodules` file reveals `nvim-treesitter` lacks a specified branch. The command defaults to `origin/HEAD` or the remote's default branch. Upstream `nvim-treesitter` appears to default to `master`.

Setting `branch = main` in `.gitmodules` for `nvim-treesitter` will influence which branch is checked out on update.

19: --- @param name string Name of the artifact to be used when requiring it (e.g. 'blink_cmp_fuzzy')
20: --- @param commit_hash? string Commit hash of the repository. If omitted, resulting filename will be `lib$name.so` instead of `lib$name.so.hash`.
21: --- @return string library_path
22: function native.library_path(repo_root, name, commit_hash)
23:   repo_root = vim.fs.normalize(repo_root)
24:   local path = repo_root .. '/lib/lib' .. name .. lib_extension
25:   if commit_hash ~= nil then return path .. '.' .. commit_hash:sub(1, 7) end
26:   return path

29: --- @param name string Name of the library to resolve (e.g. 'blink_cmp_fuzzy')
30: --- @param commit_hash? string Commit hash of the library to resolve (e.g. 'e5678fe566e86553403b3129a3684389c84fafb5'). If omitted, `lib$name.so.hash` will be skipped, and only `lib$name.so` will be attempted.
31: --- @return string? library_path
32: function native.resolve(name, commit_hash)
33:   -- $runtimepath/lib/lib*.so.hash
34:   local lib_paths = commit_hash ~= nil
35:       and vim.api.nvim_get_runtime_file('lib/lib' .. name .. lib_extension .. '.' .. commit_hash:sub(1, 7), true)
36:     or {}
37:   if #lib_paths == 0 then
38:     -- fallback to $runtimepath/lib/lib*.so
39:     lib_paths = vim.api.nvim_get_runtime_file('lib/lib' .. name .. lib_extension, true)
40:   end
41: 
42:   if #lib_paths > 1 then
43:     error(
44:       'Found multiple instances of the same library ('
45:         .. name
46:         .. '). Note that the latest update moved the binary from `site/lib/*` to `$repo/lib/*`. Please remove the former: '
47:         .. table.concat(lib_paths, ', ')
48:     )
49:   end
50:   return lib_paths[1]
51: end
52: 
53: --- @param name string Name of the library to load (e.g. 'blink_cmp_fuzzy')
54: --- @param commit_hash string? Commit hash of the library to load (e.g. 'e5678fe566e86553403b3129a3684389c84fafb5'). If omitted, `lib$name.so.hash` will be skipped, and only `lib$name.so` will be attempted.
55: --- @return any library
56: function native.load(name, commit_hash)
57:   local lib_path = native.resolve(name, commit_hash)
58:   if lib_path == nil then return error('Failed to resolve library in $runtimepath/lib/: ' .. name) end
59: 
60:   -- load the library
61:   local loader, err = package.loadlib(lib_path, 'luaopen_' .. name)
62:   if err or not loader then return error('Failed to load library: ' .. err or 'unknown error') end
63:   return loader()
64: end
65: 
66: --- @param url string
67: --- @param path string Where to save the library
68: --- @param callback fun(err: string)
69: function native.download(url, path, callback)
70:   native.mkdirp(vim.fs.dirname(path))
71: 
72:   -- if file exists, rename it and fs_unlink it to mark for garbage collection
73:   local exists = vim.uv.fs_stat(path)
74:   if exists and exists.type == 'file' then
75:     local random_hash = string.format('%06x', math.random(0, 0xFFFFFF))
76:     local new_path = path .. '.old.' .. random_hash
77:     vim.uv.fs_rename(path, new_path)
78:     vim.uv.fs_unlink(new_path)
79:   end
80: 
81:   vim.net.request(url, { outpath = path }, function(err) callback(tostring(err)) end)
82: end
83:

85: --- @param path string Where to save the library
86: --- @return blink.lib.Task<nil>
87: function native.download_async(url, path)
88:   return require('blink.lib.task').wrap(function(callback) native.download(url, path, callback) end)
89: end

The script requires a git tag for `cmp.download()`, which is not available for the submodule `blink.cmp` on `main`. However, `cmp.build()` can be used to compile `blink.cmp` with release optimizations, placing the compiled library in a specific directory. It seems like the best approach.

163: --- @param repo_root string Path to the repository root or some path inside the repository
164: --- @return string commit_hash For example 'e5678fe566e86553403b3129a3684389c84fafb5'
165: function native.git_commit(repo_root)
166:   --- @param p string
167:   --- @return string?
168:   function read_file(p)
169:     local fd = vim.uv.fs_open(p, 'r', 438) -- 438 = 0666
170:     if not fd then return end
171: 
172:     local content = vim.uv.fs_read(fd, 1024, 0)
173:     vim.uv.fs_close(fd)
174:     return content
175:   end
176: 
177:   -- Read HEAD
178:   local head_path = repo_root .. '/.git/HEAD'
179:   local head_content = read_file(head_path)
180:   if not head_content then error('Failed to read ' .. head_path) end
181:   head_content = vim.trim(head_content)
182: 
183:   -- If HEAD is a direct commit hash (detached HEAD)
184:   if head_content:match('^%x+$') then return head_content end
185: 
186:   -- HEAD contains a ref, e.g. "ref: refs/heads/main"
187:   local ref = head_content:match('^ref: (.+)$')
188:   if ref then
189:     -- Try to read the loose ref file (e.g. .git/refs/heads/main)
190:     local ref_path = repo_root .. '/.git/' .. ref
191:     local ref_content = read_file(ref_path)
192:     if ref_content then return vim.trim(ref_content) end
193:   end
194: 
195:   -- Fallback to git CLI
196:   local result = vim.system({ 'git', 'rev-parse', 'HEAD' }, { cwd = repo_root }):wait(1000)
197:   if result.code ~= 0 or result.stdout == nil then error('Failed to get git commit: ' .. (result.stderr or '')) end
198:   return vim.trim(result.stdout)
199: end
200: 
201: --- Unlike `git_commit(path)`, this function will simply return `nil` if the git commit cannot be determined
202: --- @param path string Path to the repository root or some path inside the repository
203: --- @return string? commit_hash For example 'e5678fe566e86553403b3129a3684389c84fafb5'
204: function native.try_git_commit(path)
205:   local success, commit_hash = pcall(native.git_commit, path)
206:   if success then return commit_hash end
207: end

212: function native.git_tag(repo_root, match)
213:   local cmd = match and { 'git', 'describe', '--tags', '--match', match, '--abbrev=0' }
214:     or { 'git', 'describe', '--tags', '--exact-match' }
215:   local process = vim.system(cmd, { cwd = repo_root }):wait(1000)
216:   if process.code == 0 and process.stdout then return process.stdout:match('(.+)\n') end

Thinking **Reviewing `.git` detection logic** The function searches for a `.git` directory to dete... 
**Reviewing `.git` detection logic**

The function searches for a `.git` directory to determine the repository root. `vim.fs.find` is used with `type = 'directory'`. However, in Git submodules, the `.git` entry is a *file*, not a directory. The current logic will fail in submodule contexts because it's looking for the wrong file type.

Thinking **Investigating Build Procedure** The system recommends building from source on the 'main... 
**Investigating Build Procedure**

The system recommends building from source on the 'main' branch, using a specific function. Initially, the approach considers running a build or utilizing a build tool directly. It is important to inspect the function's behavior within the application. The function utilizes a specified build tool with release flags to create the native components.


**Analyzing Git Repository Root**

The function determining the repository root might be incorrectly traversing the directory structure.  Specifically, the search for `.git` is likely being limited to directories, causing it to skip the `.git` *file* within a submodule. This leads to an incorrect root path being identified for the build process.  Therefore, the build will likely fail as it's initiated from an incorrect location.


**Re-evaluating Git Repository Path**

The previous logic might be flawed as submodules store `.git` as a file, not a directory. The system incorrectly uses a function designed to only locate directories. The correct approach would be to specify either 'file' or no `type` to correctly locate the repository.

**Confirming Submodule Dependency**

It's confirmed that the module has a submodule, pointing to an external repository. This clarifies the need for building the component within the primary repository to link with the submodule. The build might be expecting to find the external library, and not finding it since the repository root is wrong.

The following code has been modified to include a line number before every line, in the format: <line_number>: <original_line>. Please note that any changes targeting the original code should remove the line number, colon, and leading space.
1: # telescope-fzf-native.nvim
2: 
3: **fzf-native** is a `c` port of **[fzf][fzf]**. It only covers the algorithm and
4: implements few functions to support calculating the score.
5: 
6: This means that the [fzf syntax](https://github.com/junegunn/fzf#search-syntax)
7: is supported:
8: 
9: | Token     | Match type                 | Description                          |
10: | --------- | -------------------------- | ------------------------------------ |
11: | `sbtrkt`  | fuzzy-match                | Items that match `sbtrkt`            |
12: | `'wild`   | exact-match (quoted)       | Items that include `wild`            |
13: | `^music`  | prefix-exact-match         | Items that start with `music`        |
14: | `.mp3$`   | suffix-exact-match         | Items that end with `.mp3`           |
15: | `!fire`   | inverse-exact-match        | Items that do not include `fire`     |
16: | `!^music` | inverse-prefix-exact-match | Items that do not start with `music` |
17: | `!.mp3$`  | inverse-suffix-exact-match | Items that do not end with `.mp3`    |
18: 
19: A single bar character term acts as an OR operator. For example, the following
20: query matches entries that start with `core` and end with either `go`, `rb`,
21: or `py`.
22: 
23: ```
24: ^core go$ | rb$ | py$
25: ```
26: 
27: This is an advantage over the more simpler `fzy` algorithm, which is also
28: available for telescope (as native component or as lua component).
29: 
30: ## Installation
31: 
32: To get **fzf-native** working, you need to build it with either `cmake` or `make`. As of now, we do not ship binaries.
33: Both install methods will be supported going forward.
34: 
35: ### CMake (Windows, Linux, MacOS)
36: 
37: This requires:
38: 
39: - CMake, and the Microsoft C++ Build Tools on Windows
40: - CMake, make, and GCC or Clang on Linux and MacOS

**Determining Library Placement Strategy**

The newly created library file requires correct placement. The `blink.lib` file contains logic for resolving library paths based on a commit hash or fallback. Examining the code suggests that the runtime path is crucial.
The function `native.resolve` attempts to locate the library based on commit hash and then falls back to a generic name match within the runtime path if the hash lookup fails. Therefore, determining the runtime path is essential to ensure the library is found.


**Investigating Library Path Resolution**

The code uses a specific library path structure, potentially including a commit hash. The `managed:build` task moves the built artifact to this calculated path. Examining the commit message "nvim: configure..." suggests a previous library placement strategy. A test will be conducted placing the library with and without the commit hash to understand the resolution logic and if this can be symlinked.

**Confirming Treesitter Error Resolution**

The "attempt to call 'install' (a nil value)" treesitter error has disappeared. +build_blink_cmp() {

+    echo "Building telescope-fzf-native..."

+      cd "${target_dir}" || exit 1
+      make
+    )
+  fi
+}

 # For broken submodules, update submodules to git committed version
 # git submodule update
Antigravity (Assistant) 2026-09-08T17:48:42Z

Root Cause Analysis

Two interacting issues caused the error and warning during startup:

  1. nvim-treesitter API Mismatch (nil value at line 149 in init.lua):
  • init.lua uses require("nvim-treesitter").install({...}), which is part of nvim-treesitter's rewritten main branch.
  • However, config/nvim/pack/plugins/start/nvim-treesitter was checked out to cf12346a on the archived master branch (v0.10.0), where install does not exist on the module table.
  • Because .gitmodules did not specify a branch for nvim-treesitter, previous submodule updates checked out upstream's default branch (master) instead of main.
  1. blink.cmp Missing Native Fuzzy Matcher:
  • blink.cmp v2 looks for its precompiled native Rust library (libblinkcmpfuzzy.dylib on macOS / libblinkcmpfuzzy.so on Linux) under lib/ in Neovim's runtime path.
  • Because plugins are managed as native packages under pack/plugins/start/ rather than via lazy.nvim, lazy's automated build hook (build = ...) never executed, causing blink.cmp to display the fallback warning on startup.

Changes Implemented

  1. Resolved nvim-treesitter Branch:
  • Checked out commit 5cb0114e on nvim-treesitter's main branch, restoring the install function and completing parser installations.
  • Configured branch = main in .gitmodules so future submodule updates track main.
  1. Built and Linked blink.cmp Native Library:
  • Compiled blink.cmp with cargo build --release and symlinked the compiled binary to lib/libblinkcmpfuzzy.dylib.
  • Verified require('blink.cmp').library_available() now returns true.
  1. Automated Cross-Platform Native Builds in submodulesupdate.sh:
  • Added purely functional helper functions (getsharedlibext, buildblinkcmp, and buildtelescope_fzf) that run within isolated subshells to automatically compile and symlink native binaries during submodule updates on both macOS and Linux.

Getting Things Done: Recommended Next Actions

Context: @mac

  • Next Action: Launch nvim in your terminal to verify that the startup warning and line 149 error are resolved.

Context: @linux

  • Next Action: Pull the latest dotfiles commit on your Linux machine and execute ./submodulesupdate.sh to compile libblinkcmpfuzzy.so and libfzf.so using your Linux toolchain:

```bash

./submodules_update.sh

```

Context: @terminal

  • Next Action: Stage and commit the updated .gitmodules and submodulesupdate.sh:

```bash

git add .gitmodules submodules_update.sh

git commit -m "build(nvim): automate blink.cmp native build and track nvim-treesitter main"

```

+get_shared_lib_ext() {
+  case "$(uname -s)" in
+    Darwin*) echo "dylib" ;;
+    *) echo "so" ;;
+  esac
+}
+
+build_blink_cmp() {
+  local target_dir="$1"
+  local ext="$2"
+  if [ -d "${target_dir}" ]; then
+    echo "Building blink.cmp native library..."
+    (
+      cd "${target_dir}" || exit 1
+      cargo build --release
+      mkdir -p lib
+      ln -sf "../target/release/libblink_cmp_fuzzy.${ext}" "lib/libblink_cmp_fuzzy.${ext}"
+    )
+  fi
+}
+
+build_telescope_fzf() {
+  local target_dir="$1"
+  if [ -d "${target_dir}" ]; then
+    echo "Building telescope-fzf-native..."
+    (
+      cd "${target_dir}" || exit 1
+      make
+    )
+  fi
+}
+
+ext="$(get_shared_lib_ext)"
+build_blink_cmp "./config/nvim/pack/plugins/start/blink.cmp" "${ext}"
+build_telescope_fzf "./config/nvim/pack/plugins/start/telescope-fzf-native.nvim"
+
 echo -e "${GREEN}finished.${NC}"

{
  "CommandLine": "cat << 'EOF' > /Users/ahadjigeorgalis/.gemini/antigravity-cli/brain/53d58b52-f99f-4f49-bf31-27dad1e4665b/scratch/test_md.py\nimport re\nimport html\n\ndef escape(text):\n    return html.escape(text, quote=True)\n\ndef render_markdown(text):\n    if not text:\n        return \"\"\n    lines = text.split(\"\\n\")\n    out = []\n    in_code = False\n    code_lang = \"\"\n    code_buf = []\n    in_list = False\n    list_type = None # 'ul' or 'ol'\n    in_table = False\n    table_rows = []\n\n    def flush_list():\n        nonlocal in_list, list_type\n        if in_list:\n            out.append(f\"</{list_type}>\")\n            in_list = False\n            list_type = None\n\n    def flush_table():\n        nonlocal in_table, table_rows\n        if in_table:\n            t_html = [\"<div class='table-wrapper'><table>\"]\n            if table_rows:\n                # First row header\n                t_html.append(\"<thead><tr>\" + \"\".join(f\"<th>{inline_format(c)}</th>\" for c in table_rows[0]) + \"</tr></thead>\")\n                if len(table_rows) > 1:\n                    t_html.append(\"<tbody>\")\n                    for r in table_rows[1:]:\n                        t_html.append(\"<tr>\" + \"\".join(f\"<td>{inline_format(c)}</td>\" for c in r) + \"</tr>\")\n                    t_html.append(\"</tbody>\")\n            t_html.append(\"</table></div>\")\n            out.append(\"\".join(t_html))\n            in_table = False\n            table_rows = []\n\n    def inline_format(s):\n        s = escape(s)\n        # bold and italic\n        s = re.sub(r'\\*\\*\\*(.*?)\\*\\*\\*', r'<strong><em>\\1</em></strong>', s)\n        s = re.sub(r'\\*\\*(.*?)\\*\\*', r'<strong>\\1</strong>', s)\n        s = re.sub(r'___(.*?)___', r'<strong><em>\\1</em></strong>', s)\n        s = re.sub(r'__(.*?)__', r'<strong>\\1</strong>', s)\n        s = re.sub(r'\\*(.*?)\\*', r'<em>\\1</em>', s)\n        s = re.sub(r'_(.*?)_', r'<em>\\1</em>', s)\n        # inline code\n        s = re.sub(r'`([^`]+)`', r'<code>\\1</code>', s)\n        # links\n        s = re.sub(r'\\[([^\\]]+)\\]\\(([^)]+)\\)', r'<a href=\"\\2\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noopener noreferrer\">\\1</a>', s)\n        return s\n\n    for line in lines:\n        if line.startswith(\"```\"):\n            if in_code:\n                out.append(f\"<pre><code class='language-{escape(code_lang)}'>\" + escape(\"\\n\".join(code_buf)) + \"</code></pre>\")\n                in_code = False\n                code_buf = []\n            else:\n                flush_list()\n                flush_table()\n                in_code = True\n                code_lang = line[3:].strip()\n                code_buf = []\n            continue\n\n        if in_code:\n            code_buf.append(line)\n            continue\n\n        # Table rows\n        if line.strip().startswith(\"|\") and line.strip().endswith(\"|\"):\n            flush_list()\n            cells = [c.strip() for c in line.strip()[1:-1].split(\"|\")]\n            # Check if separator row\n            if all(re.match(r'^:?-+:?$', c) for c in cells):\n                continue\n            if not in_table:\n                in_table = True\n                table_rows = []\n            table_rows.append(cells)\n            continue\n        else:\n            flush_table()\n\n        # Lists\n        ul_match = re.match(r'^[ \\t]*[-*+][ \\t]+(.*)', line)\n        ol_match = re.match(r'^[ \\t]*\\d+\\.[ \\t]+(.*)', line)\n        if ul_match:\n            flush_table()\n            if not in_list or list_type != 'ul':\n                flush_list()\n                in_list = True\n                list_type = 'ul'\n                out.append(\"<ul>\")\n            out.append(f\"<li>{inline_format(ul_match.group(1))}</li>\")\n            continue\n        elif ol_match:\n            flush_table()\n            if not in_list or list_type != 'ol':\n                flush_list()\n                in_list = True\n                list_type = 'ol'\n                out.append(\"<ol>\")\n            out.append(f\"<li>{inline_format(ol_match.group(1))}</li>\")\n            continue\n        else:\n            flush_list()\n\n        # Headers\n        h_match = re.match(r'^(#{1,6})[ \\t]+(.*)', line)\n        if h_match:\n            flush_table()\n            level = len(h_match.group(1))\n            out.append(f\"<h{level}>{inline_format(h_match.group(2))}</h{level}>\")\n            continue\n\n        # Horizontal rule\n        if re.match(r'^[ \\t]*[-*_]{3,}[ \\t]*$', line):\n            flush_table()\n            out.append(\"<hr>\")\n            continue\n\n        # Blockquote\n        if line.startswith(\">\"):\n            flush_table()\n            content = line.lstrip(\"> \").strip()\n            out.append(f\"<blockquote>{inline_format(content)}</blockquote>\")\n            continue\n\n        # Normal line\n        stripped = line.strip()\n        if not stripped:\n            continue\n        out.append(f\"<p>{inline_format(stripped)}</p>\")\n\n    flush_list()\n    flush_table()\n    if in_code:\n        out.append(f\"<pre><code class='language-{escape(code_lang)}'>\" + escape(\"\\n\".join(code_buf)) + \"</code></pre>\")\n\n    return \"\\n\".join(out)\n\nprint(\"Test output:\")\nprint(render_markdown(\"### Heading\\n\\nSome text with **bold** and `code` and [link](file:///path).\\n\\n```python\\nprint('hello')\\n```\"))\nEOF\npython3 /Users/ahadjigeorgalis/.gemini/antigravity-cli/brain/53d58b52-f99f-4f49-bf31-27dad1e4665b/scratch/test_md.py\n",

  flush_list()\n            cells = [c.strip() for c in line.strip()[1:-1].split(\"|\")]\n            if all(re.match(r'^:?-+:?$', c) for c in cells):\n                continue\n            if not in_table:\n                in_table = True\n                table_rows = []\n            table_rows.append(cells)\n            continue\n        else:\n            flush_table()\n\n        # Lists\n        ul_match = re.match(r'^[ \\t]*[-*+][ \\t]+(.*)', line)\n        ol_match = re.match(r'^[ \\t]*\\d+\\.[ \\t]+(.*)', line)\n        if ul_match:\n            flush_table()\n            if not in_list or list_type != 'ul':\n                flush_list()\n                in_list = True\n                list_type = 'ul'\n                out.append(\"<ul>\")\n            out.append(f\"<li>{inline_format(ul_match.group(1))}</li>\")\n            continue\n        elif ol_match:\n            flush_table()\n            if not in_list or list_type != 'ol':\n                flush_list()\n                in_list = True\n                list_type = 'ol'\n                out.append(\"<ol>\")\n            out.append(f\"<li>{inline_format(ol_match.group(1))}</li>\")\n            continue\n        else:\n            flush_list()\n\n        # Headers\n        h_match = re.match(r'^(#{1,6})[ \\t]+(.*)', line)\n        if h_match:\n            flush_table()\n            level = len(h_match.group(1))\n            out.append(f\"<h{level}>{inline_format(h_match.group(2))}</h{level}>\")\n            continue\n\n        # HR\n        if re.match(r'^[ \\t]*[-*_]{3,}[ \\t]*$', line):\n            flush_table()\n            out.append(\"<hr>\")\n            continue\n\n        # Blockquote\n        if line.startswith(\">\"):\n            flush_table()\n            content = line.lstrip(\"> \").strip()\n            out.append(f\"<blockquote>{inline_format(content)}</blockquote>\")\n            continue\n\n        # Paragraph\n        stripped = line.strip()\n        if not stripped:\n            continue\n        out.append(f\"<p>{inline_format(stripped)}</p>\")\n\n    flush_list()\n    flush_table()\n    if in_code:\n        out.append(f\"<pre class='code-block'><code class='language-{escape(code_lang)}'>\" + escape(\"\\n\".join(code_buf)) + \"</code></pre>\")\n\n    return \"\\n\".join(out)\n\ndef parse_user_content(content_raw: str) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Extracts user request and metadata from raw input text.\"\"\"\n    req_match = re.search(r'<USER_REQUEST>\\s*(.*?)\\s*</USER_REQUEST>', content_raw, re.DOTALL)\n    request_text = req_match.group(1).strip() if req_match else content_raw.strip()\n    \n    meta_match = re.search(r'<ADDITIONAL_METADATA>\\s*(.*?)\\s*</ADDITIONAL_METADATA>', content_raw, re.DOTALL)\n    metadata_text = meta_match.group(1).strip() if meta_match else \"\"\n\n    settings_match = re.search(r'<USER_SETTINGS_CHANGE>\\s*(.*?)\\s*</USER_SETTINGS_CHANGE>', content_raw, re.DOTALL)\n    settings_text = settings_match.group(1).strip() if settings_match else \"\"\n\n    return {\n        \"request\": request_text,\n        \"metadata\": metadata_text,\n        \"settings\": settings_text\n    }\n\ndef format_timestamp(iso_str: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Formats ISO datetime string into human readable format.\"\"\"\n    if not iso_str:\n        return \"\"\n    try:\n        # e.g. 2026-09-08T13:39:09-04:00\n        dt = datetime.fromisoformat(iso_str)\n        return dt.strftime(\"%b %d, %Y %I:%M:%S %p %Z\")\n    except Exception:\n        return iso_str\n\ndef pair_events(events: list) -> list:\n    \"\"\"\n    Pairs PLANNER_RESPONSE (with tool_calls) with subsequent GENERIC (tool outputs) or SYSTEM_MESSAGE.\n    Returns structured event items.\n    \"\"\"\n    paired = []\n    i = 0\n    n = len(events)\n    while i < n:\n        item = events[i]\n        itype = item.get(\"type\")\n        \n        if itype == \"PLANNER_RESPONSE\":\n            thinking = item.get(\"thinking\", \"\")\n            tool_calls = item.get(\"tool_calls\", [])\n            content = item.get(\"content\", \"\")\n            \n            # Check if next item is the tool result\n            tool_results = []\n            j = i + 1\n            while j < n and events[j].get(\"type\") in (\"GENERIC\", \"SYSTEM_MESSAGE\"):\n                tool_results.append(events[j])\n                j += 1\n            \n            paired.append({\n                \"type\": \"planner_step\",\n                \"thinking\": thinking,\n                \"tool_calls\": tool_calls,\n                \"content\": content,\n                \"results\": tool_results,\n                \"timestamp\": item.get(\"created_at\", \"\")\n            })\n            i = j\n        elif itype == \"SYSTEM_MESSAGE\":\n            paired.append({\n                \"type\": \"system_message\",\n                \"content\": item.get(\"content\", \"\"),\n                \"timestamp\": item.get(\"created_at\", \"\")\n            })\n            i += 1\n        else:\n            paired.append({\n                \"type\": \"other\",\n                \"record\": item,\n                \"timestamp\": item.get(\"created_at\", \"\")\n            })\n            i += 1\n    return paired\n\ndef render_tool_call_html(tc: dict, results: list) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Renders a single tool call with its arguments and results.\"\"\"\n    tool_name = tc.get(\"name\", \"tool\")\n    args = tc.get(\"args\", {})\n    summary = args.get(\"toolSummary\") or orchestrator.\"\"\"\n    with open(transcript_path, 'r', encoding='utf-8') as f:\n        records = [json.loads(line) for line in f]\n\n    # Group into turns\n    turns = []\n    current_turn = None\n\n    for record in records:\n        rtype = record.get(\"type\")\n        if rtype == \"USER_INPUT\":\n            if current_turn:\n                turns.append(current_turn)\n            current_turn = {\"user\": record, \"events\": [], \"assistant\": None}\n        elif current_turn is not None:\n            if rtype == \"PLANNER_RESPONSE\" and record.get(\"content\") and not
